## no-event-time-decay-fade (intermediate) — 2026-04-11 [historical — superseded by sophisticated, cycle 50]
- **Works when:** Binary PM contract pricing a non-scheduled event modelable as Poisson arrival process (geopolitics_military, legislative_passage, executive_action, diplomatic_agreement); YES_market ∈ [0.08, 0.35]; days_remaining ≤ 21; actuarial_ratio = YES_market / actuarial_yes(λ_cat, T) ≥ 1.30 (Mode B) or ≥ 1.50 (Mode A, T ≤ 14d); no positive catalyst (< 2 relevant articles in 72h; no wire/agency breaking news); market question does NOT contain scheduled event keywords ("vote on", "election on", "scheduled meeting", "debate on [date]", "hearing on", "expires on"); liquidity ≥ $5k; bid-ask spread ≤ 0.04
- **Fails when:** Scheduled event (known resolution date — Poisson inapplicable, actuarial model breaks down); YES < 0.08 (FLB prim is the correct mechanism; subeconomic return barrier shuts out professional arbitrage); λ estimate wrong for category (miscalibrated λ inverts signal direction — most dangerous failure mode); breaking news cascade active (Poisson independence assumption violated); YES already partially decayed past actuarial_ratio threshold (signal fired late, overpricing compressed); bursty political news cycle < 72h (catalyst gate fails to exclude noise); market liquidity < $5k; Gamma API calibration scan shows overpricing_ratio < 1.30 in all YES buckets (mechanism absent)
- **Best pair(s):** Geopolitics, politics, legislative (non-calendar), executive action markets on Polymarket — NOT crypto/sports; NOT FOMC or legislative calendar items; NOT contracts with explicit resolution deadline in question text
- **Best timeframe:** Entry in final ≤ 21 days (Mode B) or ≤ 14 days (Mode A); hold to resolution or catalyst invalidation; no active management required (θ works passively)
- **Key numbers:** Mode A: actuarial_ratio ≥ 1.50, T ≤ 14d; Mode B: actuarial_ratio ≥ 1.30, T ≤ 21d; YES range 0.08–0.35 (hard boundaries); λ priors: geopolitics_military 0.003/day, legislative_passage 0.005/day, executive_action 0.008/day, diplomatic_agreement 0.002/day; calibration gates: n ≥ 50/bucket Gamma scan + λ MLE RMSE ≤ 0.02; signal frequency: 20–50/year; N=30 achievable 8–18 months; α=0.10 Kelly until calibration
- **Critical findings:** (1) Two-mode structure formalises signal gradient — Mode A is the high-conviction beachhead (T≤14, ratio≥1.50); Mode B is the broader net. Combined frequency 20–50/year makes N=30 achievable. (2) YES lower bound raised to 0.08 from naive: below 0.08 = FLB territory (subeconomic return barrier shuts out professional arb; different mechanism). The two prims are non-overlapping by construction. (3) Mechanism has two layers: cognitive (Kahneman-Tversky anchoring — retail traders fail to decay YES as T shrinks) + market structure (Ottaviani-Sørensen: in YES 0.08–0.35, per-contract NO return ≥ $0.65, professional arb CAN enter but anchoring prevents convergence in the entry window available). (4) Scheduled event keyword veto is structurally critical — Poisson assumption breaks for known-date events; without the veto, λ miscalibration is systematic. (5) λ calibration methodology formalised: MLE via Gamma API resolved market cohorts, minimize residual vs empirical resolution rate, n≥50 and RMSE≤0.02 required. (6) Snowberg & Wolfers (2010) provides the structural misperception model (γ≈0.65) that explains why cognitive anchoring creates a measurable and persistent actuarial ratio divergence. (7) Competitive moat: requires actuarial model + λ calibration; not visual; execution window hours-to-days; < estimated 10 systematic operators globally.
- **Evidence:** Kahneman & Tversky (1974, Science — anchoring and adjustment), Manski (2006, JFE — Intrade probability persistence), Snowberg & Wolfers (2010, AER — misperception model γ≈0.65 for PM structural deviation), Ottaviani & Sørensen (2008, JFE — subeconomic return barrier at extreme tails), Thaler & Ziemba (1988, JEP — cross-market temporal anchoring in parimutuel betting), Wolfers & Zitzewitz (2006, NBER WP 10504 — PM calibration deviations), Moontower APY/time-to-resolution empirical fingerprint; no own-data trades
- **Anti-prim escape hatches:** (A) Gamma scan returns overpricing_ratio < 1.30 in all [0.08–0.35, final 14d] buckets with n ≥ 50/bucket → mechanism absent → retire. (B) λ_MLE calibrated against resolved markets shows predicted actuarial_yes LOWER than empirical resolution frequency in any category → wrong-direction λ → recalibrate before deployment. (C) Live NO WR < 52% over first 30 Mode A trades → stop, run escape hatch A scan, reassess λ. (D) < 10 qualifying Mode A events/year × 2 consecutive years → participants have learned to decay YES → suspend and reassess.
- **Implementation gaps:** (1) `src/strategies/time_decay_fade.py` — not yet created; two-mode logic, λ lookup, keyword veto, catalyst gate; (2) `calibrate_lambda()` pipeline — Gamma API resolved market pull, MLE optimisation (BLOCKING gate 2); (3) `gamma_calibration_scan()` — BLOCKING gate 1 (n threshold raised to 50/bucket); (4) Catalyst velocity gate — NLP news feed or Perplexity API; manual acceptable for first 20 signals; (5) `is_scheduled_event()` helper — regex keyword veto on market question text; (6) Integration with `fractional-kelly-sizing` sophisticated (α=0.10 floor until gate 2 calibration passes)
- **Last validated:** never (cycle 49 RESEARCH; intermediate elevation from naive; two-mode structure [Mode A: ratio≥1.50 T≤14; Mode B: ratio≥1.30 T≤21]; YES lower bound formalised at 0.08; scheduled event keyword veto added; λ MLE calibration methodology formalised; 7-source basis [+4 new: Snowberg-Wolfers 2010, Ottaviani-Sørensen 2008, Thaler-Ziemba 1988, Wolfers-Zitzewitz 2006]; anti-prim D added [frequency collapse]; BLOCKING: Gamma scan n≥50 + λ MLE RMSE≤0.02)

- **Fails when:** Scheduled event with known calendar date (Poisson inapplicable — keyword veto must catch; "vote on", "election on", "deadline on", "by [month] [year]", etc.); YES < 0.08 (FLB territory — different mechanism); λ wrong direction (λ_MLE produces actuarial_yes LOWER than empirical resolution rate — inverts signal); breaking news cascade in prior 72h (Poisson independence assumption violated); actuarial_ratio already declining from above threshold (signal was late — overpricing partially corrected); Gamma scan fails Gate 1 (overpricing_ratio < 1.30 in all buckets — mechanism absent); Gate 3 paper trading WR < 52% over 30 Mode A signals

- **Best timeframe:** Entry in final ≤ 21 days (Mode B) or ≤ 14 days (Mode A); hold to resolution or catalyst invalidation; no active management (theta works passively); execution window hours-to-days (not latency-sensitive)
- **Key numbers:** Mode A: actuarial_ratio ≥ 1.50, T ≤ 14d; Mode B: actuarial_ratio ≥ 1.30, T ≤ 21d; YES range 0.08–0.35 (hard); Expected return: ratio=1.30/YES=0.20 → 5.8% gross; ratio=1.50/YES=0.20 → 13.9% gross; λ priors (with CI): geopolitics_military 0.003/day [0.0018–0.0042], legislative 0.005/day [0.003–0.008], executive 0.008/day [0.005–0.012], diplomatic 0.002/day [0.001–0.003]; conservative-use rule: λ_UPPER until n≥200; 3-gate deployment sequence; α=0.10 Kelly floor → 0.25 at n≥200, RMSE≤0.015; signal frequency: 20–50/year combined; N=30 in 8–18 months; competitive moat: < 5 systematic operators globally
- **Critical findings:** (1) Expected return formula fully derived: `NO_return = (ratio−1)×YES / (ratio×(1−YES))`; return table spans 2.6%–45.5% gross across the ratio/YES space; all category fees clear at ratio=1.30. CI bounds added; conservative-use rule (λ_UPPER) applies during pre-calibration phase. (5) Saturation threshold identified: additional operators would compress actuarial_ratio at signal fires; threshold would shift to ~1.20 before mechanism eliminates (not a discontinuous collapse). (6) Two new academic sources added: Berg et al. (2008 Handbook) — IEM price-path lifecycle evidence; Cen, Hillary & Wei (2013 JFE) — anchoring magnitude in financially-incentivised markets (15–30% insufficient adjustment), bridging lab anchoring to PM behaviour.

(2008, Handbook — IEM price-path lifecycle), Cen, Hillary & Wei (2013, JFE — anchoring magnitude 15–30% in financially-incentivised markets), Moontower APY/time-to-resolution empirical fingerprint; no own-data trades
- **Anti-prim escape hatches:** (A) Gamma scan returns overpricing_ratio < 1.30 in all three YES buckets [0.08–0.35, final 14d] with n ≥ 50/bucket → mechanism not present at detectable magnitude → do not deploy. (B) λ_MLE shows actuarial_yes LOWER than empirical resolution frequency in any category → wrong-direction λ → recalibrate; do not deploy that category. (C) Live NO WR < 52% over first 30 Mode A trades → halt, run escape hatch A scan, examine catalyst gate false negatives. (D) < 10 qualifying Mode A events/year × 2 consecutive years → participants have learned to decay YES → mechanism compressing → suspend and reassess.

## resolution-confirmation-arbitrage (sophisticated) — 2026-04-12
- **Works when:** ≥ 2 independent wire sources (Reuters/AP/Bloomberg) confirm binary outcome AND gap_age ≤ 90s AND semantic_class = "W" (W1 or W2 via semantic_risk.py) AND no active UMA dispute pre-entry; **Mode A**: confirmed-side price < $0.85, T+5–60 min post-wire (optimal T+5–T+20 min), dispute_risk < 0.03, net_return ≥ 5%; **Mode B**: confirmed-side price < $0.94, oracle dispute window active, T+60 min+, dispute_risk < 0.02, net_return ≥ 3%; single-leg binary (neg_risk=False); liquidity ≥ $5k; bid-ask ≤ $0.06; geopolitics (0% fee) or politics (4% fee) only
- **Fails when:** < 2 independent wire sources; gap_age > 90s (stale gap = semantic trap; Tier 1 evaluated and passed); semantic_class = "X" or "Y"; UMA dispute active at entry or filed post-entry; crypto or sports (6–8% dispute base rate); neg_risk=True; price above mode threshold; net_return below floor; N_eff under-counted for correlated election markets (multiple markets on same election night, ρ ≈ 0.6–0.8)
- **Best pair(s):** Geopolitics W1 (0% fee, 0.5% dispute rate — major AP/Reuters race calls); Politics W1 elections and legislative votes (1.0–1.2%); W2 Mode A only for named events (2.0–3.0%)
- **Best timeframe:** Mode A prime: T+5–T+20 min (highest EV per dollar, 70–80% gap remaining); Mode A tail: T+20–T+60 min; Mode B: T+60 min to oracle close; hold to oracle settlement ($1.00); exit: oracle settlement OR post-entry dispute filed
- **Key numbers:** Mode A gate $0.85 (extended from $0.88); Mode B gate $0.94; gap staleness 90s; min wire sources 2; Mode A dispute threshold 3%; Mode B dispute threshold 2%; Mode A floor 5%; Mode B floor 3%; EV formula: `(1-p)×(1 - 1.1×dispute_rate) - fee_rate×p×(1-p)`; convergence decay: 70–80% gap at T+5, 30–50% at T+30, 10–25% at T+60; signal frequency 90–140/year; N=30 in 2–4 months; Kelly α=0.10 floor until N≥30; N_eff = N/(1+(N−1)ρ) for correlated elections; anti-prim (C) threshold tightened to $0.93 (from $0.95)
- **Evidence:** Rodríguez et al. (2025, arxiv 2508.03474 — 75% PM arb within 1h, $40M extracted, tier competition model); Della Vedova (2025, SSRN 6191618 — 2.52c/contract bot advantage, Tier 1 T+0 window); Tetlock (2004, JLEO 20(2) — PM convergence efficiency, second-wave information processing); Berg, Nelson & Rietz (2008, Handbook Experimental Economics Results — PM competitive structure); UMA Protocol (2024 oracle docs — 24–48h challenge period); Gürkaynak, Sack & Swanson (2005, AER — implementation delay mechanism); financial-market-lead-lag sophisticated (Mode A/B architecture); cross-venue-semantic-arb sophisticated (Type W/X/Y); fractional-kelly-sizing sophisticated (α=0.10, N_eff); no own-data trades
- **Anti-prim escape hatches:** (A) WR < 85% over first 20 Type W trades → classifier leaking X/Y → halt + audit. (B) Observed dispute rate > 10% in any event class over 20 trades → exclude that class permanently. (C) Median entry price at T+5 min > $0.93 (tightened from $0.95 — Mode A gate now $0.85, so $0.93 median means Tier 2a fully consumed) over 20 consecutive events → Mode B only; reassess Mode A architecture.
- **Implementation gaps:** (1) `src/strategies/resolution_arb.py` — Mode A gate $0.85, gap_age ≤ 90s, min_sources=2, W1/W2 routing, N_eff Kelly [DEPENDS ON items 2–6]; (2) `src/utils/uma_oracle_monitor.py` — real-time UMA dispute + per-market window duration [BLOCKING]; (3) `src/feeds/news_wire_monitor.py` — Reuters+AP+Bloomberg feed, deduplication, earliest timestamp for gap_age [BLOCKING]; (4) `src/classifiers/wire_to_contract_matcher.py` — W1/W2 sub-classification, dual-source fusion, confidence ≥ 0.90 [BLOCKING]; (5) `src/risk/dispute_risk_scorer.py` — W2 multiplier 1.5× base rate; (6) `src/risk/kelly.py` — add N_eff correlated-position adjustment; (7) Reuse `src/classifiers/semantic_risk.py`, `src/risk/kelly.py`, `src/utils/uma_oracle_monitor.py`
- **Last validated:** cycle 55 sophisticated elevation; four upgrades over intermediate: [1] Mode A gate $0.88→$0.85 (EV at $0.85: 14.4% politics, 15.0% geopolitics — above 5% floor even at 1% dispute rate); [2] gap staleness gate 90s (stale gap = Tier 1 semantic reject signal); [3] dual wire source requirement (single-source retraction ~0.2%; dual reduces to ~0.0004%); [4] convergence speed model (empirical decay curve: 70–80% at T+5, 30–50% at T+30, 10–25% at T+60); added explicit EV formula; signal frequency 90–140/year; W1/W2 sub-classification; N_eff correlated-election correction; anti-prim (C) tightened $0.95→$0.93; 2 new sources (Tetlock 2004 JLEO, Berg/Nelson/Rietz 2008); 8-source total basis; BLOCKING: wire_to_contract_matcher.py (confidence ≥ 0.90), uma_oracle_monitor.py, news_wire_monitor.py

## resolution-confirmation-arbitrage (intermediate) — 2026-04-12
- **Works when:** Wire service (Reuters/AP/Bloomberg) confirms binary outcome AND semantic_class = "W" (via semantic_risk.py) AND no active UMA dispute pre-entry; **Mode A**: confirmed-side price < $0.88, T+5–60 min post-wire, dispute_risk_score < 0.03, net_return ≥ 5% (`(1−p) − fee_rate×p×(1−p)`); **Mode B**: confirmed-side price < $0.94, oracle dispute window active, T+60 min+ post-wire, dispute_risk_score < 0.02, net_return ≥ 3%; contract is single-leg binary (neg_risk=False); liquidity ≥ $5k; bid-ask ≤ $0.06; category is geopolitics (0% fee) or politics (4% fee)
- **Fails when:** semantic_class = "X" or "Y" (wire confirms fact ≠ oracle resolution criteria — primary loss mode); UMA dispute filed at or post-entry (converts near-certain convergence to directional exposure); crypto or sports category (dispute base rate 6–8%, above both mode thresholds); neg_risk multi-market bundle; price at entry ≥ mode threshold (Mode A ≥ $0.88; Mode B ≥ $0.94); net_return below mode floor after exact fee formula; dispute_risk_score at or above mode threshold (named political events 2.0–3.0% excluded from Mode B)
- **Best pair(s):** Geopolitics (0% fee, lowest dispute base rate — major elections 0.5%); Politics elections (AP/Reuters race call); Politics legislative votes (1.0–1.2% base rate); Politics appointments
- **Best timeframe:** Mode A: T+5–60 min post-wire (second-wave Tier 2 window, price $0.88–$0.93); Mode B: T+60 min to oracle close (Tier 3 oracle window, price $0.93–$0.97); hold to oracle settlement ($1.00); exit trigger: oracle settlement OR UMA dispute filed post-entry
- **Key numbers:** Mode A price gate $0.88; Mode B price gate $0.94; Mode A dispute threshold 3%; Mode B dispute threshold 2%; Mode A net return floor 5% (at p=0.88, politics: 11.6%; geopolitics: 12.0%); Mode B net return floor 3% (at p=0.94, politics: 5.8%; geopolitics: 6.0%); fee formula: `fee = fee_rate × p × (1−p)` (approaches 0 near $1.00); major elections base rate 0.5%; legislative votes 1.0–1.2%; named political events 2.0–3.0% (Mode A only); crypto 6.0% / sports 8.0% (excluded); α=0.10 Kelly; BLOCKING: wire-to-contract semantic matcher confidence ≥ 0.90 + UMA oracle monitor
- **Evidence:** Rodríguez et al. (2025, arxiv 2508.03474 — 75% of PM arb executes within 1h post-resolution, $40M extracted; tier competition model grounding), Della Vedova (2025, SSRN 6191618 — 2.52c/contract bot advantage, Tier 1 T+0 window characterisation), UMA Protocol (2024 oracle docs — 24–48h optimistic oracle challenge period), Gürkaynak, Sack & Swanson (2005, AER — second-wave implementation delay mechanism), financial-market-lead-lag sophisticated (Mode A/B architecture precedent), cross-venue-semantic-arb sophisticated (Type W/X/Y classification precedent); no own-data trades
- **Anti-prim escape hatches:** (A) WR < 85% over first 20 Type W trades → semantic_risk.py leaking X/Y contracts → halt + audit classifier. (B) Dispute rate > 10% in any category over 20 trades → structural ambiguity in that category → exclude category from both modes. (C) Median confirmed-side price at T+5 min > $0.95 over 20 consecutive events → Mode A window saturated by Tier 1 competition → Mode B only; reassess Mode A architecture.
- **Implementation gaps:** (1) `src/strategies/resolution_arb.py` — two-mode logic, price gates, dispute_risk_score gate, net_return formula gate, T-window gate, Kelly sizing, hold-to-settlement [BLOCKING: items 2–5 first]; (2) `src/utils/uma_oracle_monitor.py` — real-time UMA dispute status + post-entry monitoring [BLOCKING]; (3) `src/feeds/news_wire_monitor.py` — Reuters/AP/Bloomberg structured wire feed, T+0 timestamp; (4) `src/classifiers/wire_to_contract_matcher.py` — wire confirmation → PM resolution criteria mapping, confidence ≥ 0.90 [BLOCKING]; (5) `src/risk/dispute_risk_scorer.py` — category base rates × complexity multiplier; (6) Reuse `src/classifiers/semantic_risk.py` (Type W/X/Y gate); (7) Reuse `src/risk/kelly.py` (α=0.10 floor)
- **Last validated:** cycle 54 intermediate elevation (upgraded from naive cycle 52); three upgrades: [1] two-mode entry structure (Mode A T+5–60 min price < $0.88; Mode B oracle window price < $0.94; separate dispute/return thresholds per mode); [2] oracle dispute probability model (category base rates × complexity multiplier; crypto 6%/sports 8% excluded; elections 0.5%; legislative 1.0–1.2%); [3] fee-adjusted net return floor using exact `p×(1−p)` formula (floors 5%/3% per mode); tier competition model formalised (Tier 1/2/3); semantic W/X/Y single-venue classification; 3 anti-prim escape hatches; 6-source basis; BLOCKING: wire-to-contract matcher + UMA oracle monitor

## llm-ensemble-probability-edge (naive) — 2026-04-12 [historical — superseded by intermediate, cycle 57]
- **Works when:** ≥ 3 frontier LLM models (GPT-4o, Claude 3.5/4, Gemini 1.5/2) queried at temperature=0.0 with ≥ 3 independent runs per model; ensemble median (median of per-model medians) diverges ≥ 12% from Polymarket YES price; question is knowledge-cutoff-safe (the correct answer has not changed in the prior 30 days — base-rate-dominated, not recent-event-dominated); category is geopolitics or politics; resolution horizon 7–60 days; α=0.10 Kelly floor (uncalibrated)
- **Fails when:** Question answer depends on events in the prior 30 days (primary failure: LLM training cutoff creates categorical blindspot, not statistical noise); prompt phrasing shifts ensemble estimate >5pp (noise exceeds threshold); ensemble inter-model spread >20pp (no consensus — no trade); category is crypto/sports/weather (base rates poorly represented in LLM training data); LLMs trained on PM data anchor to PM price rather than historical base rates (circular reference — magnitude unknown); API rate limits or latency prevent timely evaluation
- **Best pair(s):** Geopolitics (0% fee; LLMs have richest base-rate training data on military conflicts, elections, diplomatic events); politics elections and legislative outcomes
- **Best timeframe:** Not latency-sensitive; signal generation 1–5 minutes (9+ API calls); suitable for daily or 6h polling; not for intraday execution; resolution 7–60 days (LLM base-rate accuracy degrades outside this window — too short: recent information gap; too long: low-frequency events with thin base rates)
- **Key numbers:** Divergence threshold 12% (heuristic, not own-data derived); minimum 3 models; minimum 3 runs per model (9+ API calls per signal); resolution 7–60 days; α=0.10 Kelly floor; knowledge-cutoff safety window: 30 days (heuristic); certainty: hypothesis; N=0 own trades
- **Critical findings:** (1) **PolySwarm (arxiv 2604.03888)** is the direct PM-context anchor: 50-agent LLM ensemble achieves positive alpha on Polymarket at quarter-Kelly sizing — mechanism confirmed at prototype level in the target deployment context. (2) **Halawi et al. (arxiv 2402.18563, 2024)**: Zero-shot GPT-4 approaches superforecaster-level Brier scores on Metaculus political questions with horizon >14 days; underperforms on questions requiring recent information (validates both the mechanism and the knowledge-cutoff limitation). (3) **Schoenegger (arxiv 2307.15313, 2023)**: GPT-4 competitive with Metaculus community median Brier score on 14d+ horizon questions. Transfer to PM unvalidated but directionally supportive. (4) **Key distinction from superforecaster-consensus-lead**: LLM ensemble is always available, sub-minute, unconstrained by human forecaster participation. The tradeoff: LLMs cannot incorporate current-event information; human superforecasters update continuously. The two prims are complementary, not substitutable — LLMs for base-rate-dominated questions (historical frequency drives outcome); superforecasters for current-context-dominated questions (recent developments drive outcome). (5) **12% divergence threshold is heuristic**: derived from (a) typical LLM calibration noise (5–10pp prompt sensitivity) + (b) PM bid-ask friction (~2–5%) + (c) safety buffer; should be recalibrated against own-data once N≥30 signals accumulated.
- **Evidence:** PolySwarm (Schoenegger et al., 2026; arxiv 2604.03888 — 50-agent LLM ensemble positive alpha on Polymarket); Halawi et al. (2024; arxiv 2402.18563 — LLMs approaching superforecaster accuracy); Schoenegger & Schoenegger (2023; arxiv 2307.15313 — GPT-4 competitive with Metaculus community median Brier); no own-data trades
- **Anti-prim escape hatches (to be specified at intermediate elevation):** (A) Rolling calibration: ensemble median RMSE > 0.20 over first 50 evaluated questions vs resolved outcomes → mechanism not adding signal above noise → retire. (B) Live WR < 52% over first 30 trades with divergence ≥ 12% → threshold too low or knowledge-cutoff contamination → halt, audit. (C) Prompt sensitivity: if ≥ 3 equivalent phrasings of same question produce spread > 15pp across ensemble → structural instability → no trade on that question class.
- **Implementation gaps:** (1) `src/strategies/llm_ensemble_edge.py` — multi-model polling loop, temperature=0.0, 3-run median per model, ensemble median, divergence gate, knowledge-cutoff safety classifier; (2) `src/filters/knowledge_cutoff_guard.py` — classify whether question answer depends on sub-30-day information (heuristic: event keywords, recency markers, ongoing-event flags); (3) `src/risk/kelly.py` — confirm α=0.10 floor applied (reuse from fractional-kelly-sizing sophisticated); (4) LLM API clients: OpenAI (gpt-4o), Anthropic (claude-3-5-sonnet), Google (gemini-1.5-pro)
- **Last validated:** cycle 56 new prim creation; zero own-data trades; naive level; knowledge-cutoff gate is a mandatory pre-condition before any signal is evaluated; intermediate elevation requires: (a) 30-question calibration sample with own LLM queries vs resolved PM outcomes, (b) prompt sensitivity analysis, (c) knowledge-cutoff gate validation, (d) API cost model

## llm-ensemble-probability-edge (intermediate) — 2026-04-12
- **Works when (Mode A):** ≥ 3 frontier LLMs polled at temperature 0.0 with standardised CoT anti-anchoring prompt; ≥ 3 runs per model; performance-weighted ensemble estimate (or equal-weight median fallback) diverges ≥ 12% from PM YES price; knowledge-cutoff classifier score ≤ 0.2 (base-rate-dominated: recurring event type, answer determined by historical base rates, not post-cutoff developments); geopolitics category only; resolution 14–60 days; inter-model spread < 20pp; all models pass Brier ≤ 0.30 calibration gate (20+ matched historical questions); N_eff Kelly applied for same-event clusters
- **Works when (Mode B):** Same query protocol; classifier score ≤ 0.4 (reference-class plausible: specific actor/outcome question but historical analogies rich in training); geopolitics or politics-elections; resolution 14–45 days; divergence ≥ 18%; inter-model spread < 25pp; same model gate
- **Fails when:** Knowledge-cutoff score exceeds mode threshold (categorical failure — largest failure mode by mechanism); inter-model spread ≥ limit (no LLM consensus); horizon < 14 days (PM crowd outperforms LLMs at short horizons, Schoenegger 2023); horizon > 60d (Mode A) or 45d (Mode B) — declining base-rate precision on distant events; any model failed Brier gate (remove model before signal generation); category is crypto/sports/weather (base rates underrepresented in training); prompt sensitivity spread > 15pp — structural instability (Escape Hatch C); mode-specific WR < 52% / 30 trades (Escape Hatch B)
- **Best categories:** Geopolitics (Mode A primary — recurring events: elections, conflicts, treaty outcomes; LLMs have richest training data); politics-elections (Mode B secondary)
- **Key numbers:** Mode A divergence 12%; Mode B divergence 18%; knowledge-cutoff classifier score ≤ 0.2 (Mode A) / ≤ 0.4 (Mode B); horizon 14–60d (Mode A) / 14–45d (Mode B); inter-model spread limit 20pp / 25pp; model Brier gate ≤ 0.30; calibration pre-screen n ≥ 20 questions/mode; API cost ~$0.04/evaluation (~$3/year at 80 signals); signal frequency ~20–80/year (Mode A ~10–30; Mode B ~10–50); intra-event ρ̄ ≈ 0.70 for N_eff; α=0.10 Kelly floor; certainty: hypothesis; N=0 own-data trades
- **Evidence:** PolySwarm (arxiv 2604.03888 — 50-agent LLM ensemble positive alpha on PM; primary PM-context anchor); Halawi et al. (arxiv 2402.18563, 2024 — LLMs approach superforecaster accuracy; CoT improves calibration → grounds standardised CoT prompt); Schoenegger & Schoenegger (arxiv 2307.15313, 2023 — GPT-4 competitive at 14d+ horizon; underperforms short-horizon → grounds 14d floor); Atanasov et al. (2024 — performance-weighted aggregation outperforms CDA → grounds model weighting design)
- **Anti-prim escape hatches:** (A) RMSE > 0.20 / 50 scored questions per mode → halt that mode, recalibrate thresholds; (B) live WR < 52% / 30 closed trades combined → full halt, audit mechanism; (C) prompt sensitivity spread > 15pp on 5 controlled variants → halt, redesign prompts, raise threshold +3pp; (D) model rolling Brier > 0.30 / 20 calibration questions → remove model from ensemble, require replacement
- **Implementation gaps:** `knowledge_cutoff_guard.py` Gate 3 entity-status lookup (BLOCKING — required before Mode B can operate); calibration pre-screen 20+ matched historical questions per mode (BLOCKING deployment); `llm_calibration.py` Brier weighting module; N_eff extension in `kelly.py`; prompt injection sanitisation in question pre-processing; `llm_ensemble_edge.py` two-mode routing logic
- **Last validated:** cycle 57 intermediate elevation; naive superseded; zero own-data trades; certainty: hypothesis; calibration pre-screen BLOCKING live deployment; Mode A is primary target for calibration pre-screen (tighter conditions, stronger mechanism)

## political-hedge-instrument-signal (naive) — 2026-04-12
- **Works when:** Pre-registered instrument-event pair (instrument-event registry Gate 1 required); instrument moves ≥ 2% in 4h window; no earnings/fundamental/central-bank catalyst within catalyst exclusion window (instrument-class specific: MXN — no FOMC ±24h, no Mexico CPI/GDP ±6h, no EM contagion (VIX spike ≥5pts in 1h); defense ETF — no top-5 holding earnings ±24h, no sector-wide move >1%); equivalent PM YES price has NOT moved proportionally in instrument-predicted direction; PM category is geopolitics or politics; liquidity ≥ $5k; α=0.10 Kelly floor
- **Fails when:** Instrument move driven by non-political catalyst (confounders — PRIMARY failure mode: MXN responds to EM capital flows, Fed expectations, Mexico macro data independently of US election; requires catalyst exclusion filter); instrument-event registry not built (BLOCKING pre-condition — no signals valid without verified pairings); causal direction inverted (BTC/crypto regulation: PM whale concentration may cause BTC move, not vice versa — direction verification required per pairing); resolution criteria divergence (PM question framing does not match binary the financial instrument is hedging — same structural failure as financial-market-lead-lag limitation #1); illiquid instrument (EM sovereign bonds, small-cap sector ETFs — real-time data unavailable); PM already caught up (instrument signal already priced into PM by time of signal detection — lag window expired)
- **Best categories:** Geopolitics (0% fee) × US presidential elections (MXN/USD pairing — highest historical evidence quality); geopolitics × military escalation markets (defense ETF basket pairing)

## news-velocity-informed-directional (naive) — 2026-04-12
- **Works when:** News article publication velocity about the market event exceeds 3× the 24-hour rolling baseline rate within a 30-minute window AND sentiment direction is non-neutral (clearly positive or clearly negative); market resolution > 4h (insufficient lag for velocity → PM update cycle in near-expiry markets); PM market liquidity ≥ $3k; geopolitics or politics category (lower fee friction — 0% taker on geopolitics)
- **Fails when:** Professional bots (PolySwarm, Polymarket Research) consuming same news feed sub-30s — the institutional lag may already be closed before the 30-minute window triggers; news spike is clickbait/tabloid rather than authoritative news source (source quality absent at naive level); velocity spike confirms already-priced event (institutional front-running complete; PM already reflects news); sentiment direction is genuinely ambiguous (mixed expert interpretation of same event — e.g., "ceasefire deal" interpreted as YES or NO for "conflict ongoing"); entity matching failure — article count includes tangentially related events that don't map to the PM contract; fee dominance: 5% geopolitics taker fee consumes ~4pp at p=0.50, requiring ≥9pp directional move within 90-minute window for positive EV; velocity definition not calibrated — 30-minute window and 3× threshold are heuristics with no own-data derivation
- **Best categories:** Geopolitics (0% taker fee — most viable despite shallow institutional lag); politics (fee = 0.07×P×(1−P) at Kalshi → not applicable; Polymarket politics fee structure varies)
- **Best timeframe:** Real-time monitoring; 30-minute velocity window; 90-minute hold max
- **Key numbers:** Velocity threshold 3× (heuristic; uncalibrated); baseline window 24h rolling (heuristic); hold max 90 min (heuristic); institutional reaction lag: 5–15 min (hypothesis, not measured); retail PM participant lag: 30–120 min (Cowgill & Zitzewitz 2015 ReStat inferred); fee floor at p=0.50 geopolitics: ~5% → requires ≥9pp move for positive EV; 0 own-data trades
- **Academic anchors:** Tetlock (2007 JF) — news sentiment predicts next-day equity returns (equity, not PM); Cowgill & Zitzewitz (2015 ReStat) — prediction market prices react to event news with heterogeneous lag; Ranco et al. (2015) — Twitter volume (velocity proxy) precedes prediction market price moves on political events (closest direct anchor; Twitter ≠ structured news DB)
- **Implementation gaps (5):** (1) GDELT GKG real-time feed integration (GDELT 2.0 API or streaming webhook → article count per entity per 30-min window); (2) entity-matching pipeline (GDELT themes/actors → Polymarket market title via NLP cosine similarity, ≥ 0.85 match required — BLOCKING); (3) sentiment classifier (transformer-based polarity on headline batch; distinguish genuinely positive/negative from ambiguous — BLOCKING); (4) baseline rate estimator (rolling 24h article count; day-of-week adjustment; event lifecycle phase adjustment — spike detection becomes entity-aware, not raw count); (5) velocity-to-EV model (calibrate 3× threshold and 30-min window against historical GDELT + Polymarket price data; scan over threshold ∈ [2.0, 2.5, 3.0, 4.0, 5.0] × window ∈ [15, 30, 45, 60] = 20-cell grid)
- **Anti-prim triggers:** (A) own-data WR < 53% / 30 trades (hypothesis failure); (B) median observed PM price move within 90-min window < 6pp (insufficient directional magnitude to overcome 5% fee floor); (C) 3× velocity threshold fires > 10/day on geopolitics markets (signal too frequent; baseline model is broken — entity matching too loose or velocity definition captures routine news cycles)
- **Last validated:** never (NEW naive prim — 15th polymarket prim class; information diffusion velocity axis; no implementation; all thresholds heuristic; BLOCKING: entity-matching pipeline + sentiment classifier before any own-data validation)

## anchor-event-recency-bias-fade (naive) — 2026-04-12 [historical — superseded by intermediate, cycle 76]
- **Works when:** Major resolved event in category C (political violence / geopolitical conflict / natural disaster / electoral) with GDELT anchor_article_count > 1,000 in 48h post-resolution AND outcome surprised PM consensus (resolved against ≥ 55% prior PM probability — genuine availability shock); similar target market (same category, entity_sim < 0.70 to anchor entity) repriced ≥ 8pp upward within 7 days of anchor resolution; target YES ∈ [0.15, 0.55]; target resolution > 14 days; geopolitics or politics category (fee 0–3%)
- **Fails when (PRIMARY — information vs bias confound):** YES price rise reflects genuine base-rate update from anchor event rather than availability bias — seismological activity, deteriorating geopolitical indicators, or deteriorating security conditions may legitimately raise category probability; no filter separates these at naive level; PRIMARY blocker for intermediate elevation; **if < 50% of historical target markets resolve NO after anchor-driven YES spike, retire as anti-prim**
- **Fails when (secondary):** Anchor event resolves in expected direction (PM consensus was correct → no availability shock; "surprising" gate not triggered); target market YES already in FLB range (< 0.10 → use FLB-fade prim instead); market within 14 days of resolution at signal (anchor decay window too short for meaningful entry); entity_sim ≥ 0.70 (target IS the anchor event's continued coverage — different mechanism applies; news-velocity prim more relevant); NO anchor salience: GDELT article count fails > 1,000 threshold (event was minor; insufficient cognitive imprint); target market liquidity < $3k (spread unreliable)
- **Best categories:** Natural disaster (actuarial base rates well-defined from NOAA/Swiss Re; information vs bias confound smallest when seismological/meteorological conditions are stable); political violence / military conflict (high salience; availability bias most documented for violence perception — Tversky & Kahneman 1974); electoral (post-surprising-outcome → inflation of similar-party/similar-outcome markets)
- **Worst categories:** Sports (base rates shift rapidly; anchor event often directly informative about team/player conditions — information update, not bias); economic policy (FOMC/fiscal — financial markets price in information efficiently; financial-market-lead-lag prim dominates)
- **Best timeframe:** Signal triggered within 0–7 days of anchor resolution; hold 7–14 days (availability decay window); NOT latency sensitive (cognitive recency effect decays over days, not minutes)
- **Key numbers:** Anchor salience threshold 1,000 GDELT articles/48h (heuristic; needs calibration — intended to capture 90th pctl event salience); surprising outcome gate: resolved against PM consensus ≥ 55% (to ensure genuine availability shock, not routine confirmation); target YES range [0.15, 0.55] (base-rate overpricing most visible in mid-range; FLB prim covers < 0.10); entity_sim cutoff < 0.70 (exclude direct continuations of anchor event); hold max 14 days; anchor decay gate: GDELT velocity < 0.5× post-resolution peak (recency effect dissipated); α=0.10 Kelly floor (uncalibrated mandatory); N=0 own trades; certainty: hypothesis
- **Academic anchors (4):** Tversky & Kahneman (1974 Science) — availability heuristic: judged probability correlates with ease of recall; recent dramatic events inflate similar-event frequency estimates; Kahneman & Tversky (1979 Econometrica) — Prospect Theory probability weighting γ≈0.65: recent-experience events cognitively over-sampled → subjective probability elevated above actuarial; Barber & Odean (2008 RFS) — attention-driven retail buying after salient events (glitter effect); equity reversion post-attention spike documented; Greenwood & Shleifer (2014 RFS) — extrapolation of recent dramatic experience → systematic return overestimation; analogous probability overestimation in event prediction
- **Implementation gaps (3):** (1) Event category classifier: NLP pipeline to classify PM markets into mutually exclusive event categories (political violence / geopolitical / natural disaster / electoral / regulatory) — required for "same category C" matching; shares infrastructure with semantic-correlation-pair-trade; (2) Anchor salience threshold calibration: scan GDELT GKG 2022–2026 for event salience distribution by category; set threshold at 90th percentile to distinguish major vs routine events; (3) Historical resolution database scan: identify all cases where similar-category market opened or repriced ≥ 8pp within 7 days of anchor event; check resolution outcome (YES or NO); this scan is the anti-prim validation step — if < 50% resolve NO, retire
- **Anti-prim triggers:** (A) Historical scan shows similar-category markets that triggered the entry rule resolved YES (not NO) > 50% of the time — this means anchor events are informative (updating base rate), not distorting; retire prim; (B) Own-data rolling 20-trade WR < 50% → bias mechanism absent in actual PM; (C) < 1 qualifying anchor event per quarter → signal frequency insufficient for calibration (Escape Hatch C equivalent — anti-prim by rarity)
- **Shared pipeline:** anchor event detection (GDELT article count by entity by time window) uses the same GDELT GKG infrastructure as `news-velocity-informed-directional`; pipeline development is shared; implement both entity-matching pipelines together
- **Last validated:** never (NEW naive prim — 16th polymarket prim class; cross-event availability bias signal; no implementation; 6 limitations; primary challenge is information vs bias confound; BLOCKING: event category classifier + anchor salience calibration + historical resolution scan before any own-data validation)

## anchor-event-recency-bias-fade (intermediate) — 2026-04-12 [historical — superseded by sophisticated, cycle 77]
- **Works when — Mode A (natural disaster, low confound):** GDELT anchor_article_count > 1,000/48h + anchor outcome surprised PM consensus (≥ 55% prior PM prob) + same GDELT natural-disaster category + target YES spiked ≥ 8pp within 5 days + geographic distance from anchor epicentre to target entity > 500km + NOAA active alert severity < MODERATE at target location (72h window) + USGS magnitude < 4.5 at target location (72h window) + target YES ∈ [0.15, 0.50] + resolution > 14 days + entity_sim < 0.70 + GDELT anchor velocity < 0.5× peak at entry → BUY NO; hold 10-day max; α=0.10 Kelly floor
- **Works when — Mode B (political violence/electoral, moderate confound):** Same salience/surprise gates + same GDELT political-violence or electoral category + target YES spiked ≥ 12pp within 3 days + ACLED event rate in target region < +20% delta (7d post-anchor vs 30d baseline) + anchor primary_actor ≠ target primary_actor AND anchor conflict_system_id ≠ target conflict_system_id + target YES ∈ [0.15, 0.55] + resolution > 21 days + entity_sim < 0.70 + GDELT velocity < 0.5× peak → BUY NO at 0.75× Mode A size; hold 7-day max; α=0.08 Kelly floor
- **Fails when (PRIMARY, both modes):** NOAA/USGS alert active at target location (Mode A — underlying conditions changed; genuine base-rate update, not bias); ACLED delta ≥ +20% (Mode B — security conditions deteriorating; contagion not bias); entity_sim ≥ 0.70 (target IS a continuation of anchor event)
- **Fails when (secondary):** Target YES spiked but already decayed before entry (GDELT velocity ≥ 0.5× peak at intended entry — recency effect already correcting); anchor surprise gate not triggered (PM consensus was correct — no availability shock); Mode B: distinct-trigger gate fails (same actor or same conflict network — genuine contagion, not cross-category contamination); target market liquidity < $3k (spread unreliable); resolution < minimum required days (exit window too tight)
- **Mode comparison:** Mode A (natural disaster) has lowest confound because actuarial risk is geographically bounded (earthquake in Tokyo does not change flood risk in London); NOAA/USGS gate is objectively verifiable. Mode B (political violence) has moderate confound — political events can spread via network contagion (ACLED delta gate reduces but does not eliminate confound); size discounted 25% accordingly.
- **Key numbers:** Mode A spike threshold 8pp; Mode B spike threshold 12pp (tighter window, higher bar due to confound); geographic distance cutoff 500km Mode A; Mode A hold max 10 days (Eisensee-Strömberg 2007 media decay); Mode B hold max 7 days; effect size proxy: Kunreuther et al. 1978 insurance demand +100–200% in zero-risk regions → ~3–6pp YES overpricing at p=0.20; Palm 1995: +30% insurance spike in non-epicentre counties post-Northridge; parameter grid 2,880 cells → CPCV + Deflated Sharpe correction mandatory when optimising (Bailey-Borwein-Lopez de Prado SSRN 2326253); N_eff Kelly adjustment required when ≥ 2 active anchor events in same category/14d window
- **Academic anchors (8):** (Carried) Tversky & Kahneman 1974 Science — availability heuristic; Johnson & Tversky 1983 JPSP — cross-category mood contamination; Barber & Odean 2008 RFS — attention-driven retail reversion; Greenwood & Shleifer 2014 RFS — extrapolation bias. (New) Gennaioli & Shleifer 2010 QJE — salience theory: agents systematically overweight most salient recent analog; Eisensee & Strömberg 2007 QJE — media cycle decay quantifies hold windows; Kunreuther et al. 1978 Wiley — insurance demand +100–200% in zero-risk regions post-anchor; Palm 1995 Westview — +30% earthquake insurance in non-epicentre counties (independent replication)
- **Anti-prim escape hatches (3):** (A) Historical scan (GDELT 2022–2026 + PM resolution API): if ≥ 50% of same-category targets triggered by anchor-spike criteria resolved YES → retire mode; (B) Rolling 20-trade own-data WR < 50% per mode for 2 consecutive windows → retire mode; (C) < 5 qualifying anchor events/year × 2 years per mode → lower threshold one step or retire
- **Remaining blockers for sophisticated:** (1) N_eff correlation adjustment for concurrent active anchor events (≥ 2 in same category/14d → reduce Kelly N_eff); (2) Mode-specific GDELT salience quantile calibration (1,000 article threshold is heuristic — needs 90th-percentile verification per category); (3) Entity-category NLP classifier precision/recall evaluation; (4) Sentiment velocity asymmetry gate: Gennaioli-Shleifer predicts stronger fade for low-frequency high-severity anchors vs high-frequency moderate-severity — test as additional gate at sophisticated tier
- **Shared pipeline:** GDELT GKG article count / velocity infrastructure shared with `news-velocity-informed-directional`; entity-category NLP classifier shared with `semantic-correlation-pair-trade`
- **Last validated:** never (elevated naive → intermediate, cycle 76; information vs bias confound resolved via two-mode condition-stability gates; 8 academic anchors; effect size quantified via Kunreuther/Palm insurance research; 3 formal anti-prim escape hatches; BLOCKING: anti-prim historical scan + entity-category NLP classifier before any own-data validation)

- **Fails when (primary):** ADX_4h > 35 (hard regime exclusion — forced-liquidation mechanism replaced by structural deleveraging in deep downtrends; OI can decline for weeks without reversal) | EMA200_4h slope < −2% (secondary structural-bear exclusion) | quarterly rollover window (mechanical OI decline from futures contract expiry; not positioning signal) | capitulation-exhaustion-reversal conditions active (RSI < 25 + N≥5 reds + vol > 2.5× SMA — defer to that prim)

(1996, JFM) — OI changes lead price reversals

## anchor-event-recency-bias-fade (sophisticated) — 2026-04-12
- **Works when — Mode A (natural disaster, low confound):** GDELT anchor_article_count ≥ category P90 × 0.75 (preliminary P90: natural_disaster ~1,200/48h) + anchor surprises PM (≥55% prior) + same GDELT natural-disaster category + target YES spiked ≥ tier-specific threshold (HIGH ≥6pp; MODERATE ≥8pp; LOW ≥10pp; tier = salience_ratio classification) within 5 days + geographic distance > 500km + NOAA < MODERATE + USGS < 4.5 + target YES ∈ [0.15, 0.50] + resolution > 14 days + entity_sim < 0.70 + GDELT velocity < 0.5× peak at entry + N_eff-adjusted Kelly sizing → BUY NO; hold 10-day max; α=0.10 floor
- **Works when — Mode B (political violence/electoral, moderate confound):** Same P90 gate (political_violence ~800/48h; electoral ~1,500/48h) + anchor surprise gate + YES spike ≥ tier-specific threshold (HIGH ≥10pp; MODERATE ≥12pp; LOW ≥15pp) within 3 days + ACLED delta < +20% + distinct primary_actor AND conflict_system_id + YES ∈ [0.15, 0.55] + resolution > 21 days + entity_sim < 0.70 → BUY NO at 0.75× Mode A size; hold 7-day max; α=0.08 floor
- **Salience tier classification:** salience_ratio = anchor_article_count / category_baseline_48h; HIGH = ratio ≥5.0 AND count ≥P90 (strongest fade; Lichtenstein et al. 1978 — rare dramatic events show largest judged-frequency overestimation); MODERATE = ratio ≥2.0 AND count ≥P90×0.75; LOW = below MODERATE (weak signal, only enter with own-data WR support)
- **N_eff concurrent-anchor adjustment:** ≥ 2 open positions in same GDELT category within 14 days → N_eff = N / (1 + (N−1) × 0.60); hard cap N_eff ≤ 3; per-position Kelly = full_kelly / N_eff
- **Classifier FPR adjustment:** entity-category NLP classifier FPR must be evaluated (labeled corpus ≥ 50 pairs) before deployment. If FPR > 0.15 → BLOCKING. Anti-prim historical scan WR adjusted: adjusted_pct_yes = (raw_pct_yes − FPR × 0.50) / (1 − FPR)
- **Fails when (PRIMARY, both modes):** NOAA/USGS alert active at target location (Mode A); ACLED delta ≥ +20% (Mode B); entity_sim ≥ 0.70; classifier FPR > 0.15 (deployment blocked until retrained)
- **Fails when (secondary):** GDELT velocity ≥ 0.5× peak at entry (fade already correcting); LOW-tier salience ratio without own-data WR support; N_eff > 3 concurrent same-category positions without Kelly reduction; HIGH-tier events underperforming MODERATE-tier in own-data (collapse tiers per anti-prim D)
- **Deployment gate sequence:** Gate 0 classifier FPR pre-screen (≥50 labeled pairs; FPR ≤ 0.15 required) → Gate 1 GDELT P90 calibration (query 2022–2026 archive) → Gate 2 anti-prim historical scan with FPR adjustment → Gate 3 paper trading 20 signals with tier-stratified WR → Gate 4 live at α=0.10 floor
- **Anti-prim escape hatches (4):** (A) Historical scan (GDELT + PM resolution + FPR-adjusted): if ≥ 50% resolved YES → retire mode; (B) Rolling 20-trade WR < 50% per mode / 2 windows → retire mode; (C) < 5 qualifying events/year × 2 years → lower threshold or retire; (D) HIGH-tier WR < MODERATE-tier by > 10pp for 2 windows → collapse salience tiers, revert to intermediate thresholds
- **Key numbers:** Mode A P90 ~1,200/48h (natural disaster); Mode B P90 ~800/48h (political violence), ~1,500/48h (electoral); salience_ratio boundary HIGH/MODERATE = 5.0×; MODERATE/LOW = 2.0×; ρ_anchor = 0.60 (concurrent same-category); N_eff hard cap = 3; FPR block threshold = 0.15; expected edge HIGH tier: ~5–8pp gross Mode A; parameter grid >3,000 cells → CPCV + Deflated Sharpe mandatory
- **Academic anchors (10):** (Carried 8) Tversky & Kahneman 1974 Science; Johnson & Tversky 1983 JPSP; Barber & Odean 2008 RFS; Greenwood & Shleifer 2014 RFS; Gennaioli & Shleifer 2010 QJE; Eisensee & Strömberg 2007 QJE; Kunreuther et al. 1978 Wiley; Palm 1995 Westview. (New) Lichtenstein, Slovic, Fischhoff, Layman & Combs 1978 JEPHPP — rare dramatic events show largest judged-frequency overestimation; grounds HIGH-tier salience asymmetry; Loewenstein, Weber, Hsee & Welch 2001 Psychological Bulletin — emotional risk response activates faster and decays slower than cognitive correction; supports 10–14 day fade window being emotionally driven
- **Shared pipeline:** GDELT GKG infrastructure shared with `news-velocity-informed-directional`; entity-category NLP classifier shared with `semantic-correlation-pair-trade` (FPR evaluation needed for both)
- **Last validated:** never (elevated intermediate → sophisticated, cycle 77; 4 blockers resolved: N_eff concurrent-anchor Kelly formalised, GDELT P90 category-specific calibration, NLP classifier FPR incorporated into anti-prim scan, Gennaioli-Shleifer severity-frequency salience tier added; 10 academic anchors; 4 formal anti-prim escape hatches; BLOCKING: Gate 0 classifier FPR pre-screen + Gate 1 GDELT P90 calibration + Gate 2 anti-prim historical scan — same as intermediate; N=0 own trades)

## institutional-expert-consensus-reversion (naive) — 2026-04-12 [historical — superseded cycle 80]
- **Works when:** Contract resolves on a published authoritative numerical figure (BLS CPI, BLS NFP, BEA GDP, Federal Reserve effective funds rate, NOAA temperature anomaly percentile, comparable international equivalents) AND Bloomberg/Reuters survey consensus median available for same indicator within 48h of entry AND resolution ≤ 60 days AND |consensus_implied_prob − pm_yes_price| ≥ 0.15 → fade toward consensus
- **Fails when (PRIMARY):** Consensus-to-probability conversion inaccurate (σ estimate unavailable or historically miscalibrated → implied probability misrepresents consensus signal); GDP or revised-series indicators where PM resolves on initial release and consensus forecasts the "true" figure (initial vs revised divergence contaminates signal); contract resolution criteria reference a different data vintage, seasonal adjustment method, or index basis than the consensus survey
- **Fails when (secondary):** PM is efficiently priced from institutional participants already (high liquidity > $200k, tight spread < 2% — professional arbitrageurs may have already closed the gap); consensus itself is serially biased (CPI consensus exhibits positive serial correlation — subsequent month consensus may lag; check for systematic consensus drift before trusting direction); indicator outside primary economist survey scope (niche regional indicators with few professional forecasters)
- **Key numbers:** Entry threshold = 15pp (untuned naive heuristic); exit threshold = 8pp gap (consensus update narrows gap below this); max hold = 30 days (if no resolution); resolution window gate = ≤ 60 days; α = 0.10 Kelly floor; σ fallback = historical release standard deviation over trailing 24 releases; N_eff adjustment TBD at intermediate (likely ρ ≈ 0.40–0.60 for same-category macro indicators released same month)
- **Academic anchors (6):** Ang, Bekaert & Wei 2007 JF (SPF outperforms market-implied and naive models for near-term CPI/output); Coibion & Gorodnichenko 2015 AER (SPF better calibrated than futures-implied inflation; market over-weights recent news); Romer & Romer 2000 AEA P&P (Fed Greenbook outperforms private sector for inflation and output at ≤8 quarter horizon); Tetlock 2005 "Expert Political Judgment" (domain experts outperform generalists in narrow quantitative specialties with established measurement frameworks); Silver 2012 "The Signal and the Noise" (consensus of domain expert models outperforms individual experts and prediction markets for quantitative outcomes); Grossman & Stiglitz 1980 AER (informed agents produce superior forecasts vs uninformed crowds when information acquisition costs are asymmetric — professional economists vs retail PM participants)
- **Deployment blockers:** (1) Bloomberg/Reuters consensus API access (new data dependency, not shared with any existing prim); (2) pm_resolution_mapper.py must correctly identify which external indicator release and which threshold governs each PM contract (shared blocker with resolution-confirmation-arb, financial-market-lead-lag, political-hedge-instrument-signal); (3) consensus σ calibration study (N≥30 per indicator category) must confirm conversion accuracy before trusting implied probabilities
- **Anti-prim escape hatches (pre-specified):** (A) Historical calibration scan: if consensus-implied direction predicts resolution direction in ≤ 55% of cases across ≥ 30 events per indicator category → close that category; (B) Consensus σ miscalibration: if Brier score on N(·) conversion > 0.25 (N≥30) → recalibrate σ or suspend prim; (C) Own-data WR < 55% at N≥30 for 2 windows → anti-prim
- **Shared pipeline:** pm_resolution_mapper.py (shared with resolution-confirmation-arb, financial-market-lead-lag, political-hedge-instrument-signal); no other shared dependencies — Bloomberg/Reuters consensus API is net-new
- **Path to intermediate:** Resolve Limitation #1 (consensus-to-probability conversion calibration): pull Bloomberg/Reuters historical consensus archive (σ fields) for CPI/NFP/GDP 2018–2025; compute N(·)-based implied probabilities; compare direction vs resolution on all contemporaneous PM contracts (N≥30 per category); target ≥ 60% directional accuracy; add category-specific divergence thresholds calibrated to historical σ magnitude
- **Last validated:** never (NEW naive prim — 17th polymarket prim class, 8th signal axis; cycle 79; zero own trades; academic grounding strong for calibration advantage claim; consensus-to-probability conversion accuracy is the unresolved naive-level blocker)

- **Academic anchors (6):** Ballis & Drakos 2020 FRL (crypto herding); Vidal-Tomás/Ibáñez/Farinós 2019 FRL (transaction-level herding); De Long/Shleifer/Summers/Waldmann 1990 JPE (noise trader theory); Baker & Wurgler 2006 JF (sentiment contrarian baseline); Brunnermeier & Pedersen 2009 RFS (dual-gate theory); Kumar & Lee 2006 JF (carried)

## conditional-probability-cascade-arbitrage (naive) — 2026-04-12 [cycle 114]
- **Works when:** Polymarket lists upstream market U and downstream market D where D logically requires U (strong cascade: P(D=YES | U=NO) ≈ 0); P(D)_market diverges from P(U)_market × r by > CASCADE_GAP_THRESHOLD (0.08) in either direction; r estimable from ≥ 15 comparable historical instances (US presidential primary-general, legislative passage cascade, sports tournament) or from structural logic; both legs have liquidity ≥ $5k, bid-ask ≤ $0.05; resolution timeline allows ≥ 7 days between upstream and downstream resolution
- **Fails when:** Cascade is weak (P(D=YES | U=NO) >> 0 — third-party candidate scenarios, weak conditional dependency); r is unknown or highly uncertain (CI width > 0.20 — no comparable historical instances; unprecedented structural features); liquidity imbalance (downstream < $1k depth); both markets in FLB zone; genuine conditional-independent information about D (downstream-specific event changes P(D | U=YES) rather than P(U)); upstream already resolved
- **Mechanism:** Conjunction fallacy (Tversky & Kahneman 1983) — traders price U and D in cognitive isolation, violating the Bayesian subset axiom P(D) ≤ P(U). Hard convergence at upstream resolution provides deterministic exit not present in correlation-based prims.
- **Distinct from:**
  - cross-venue-semantic-arb: same event, different venues
  - semantic-correlation-pair-trade: bidirectional correlation, no Bayesian constraint, no deterministic forcing event
  - binary-arb-completeness: single-market P(YES)+P(NO)=1
  - base-rate-neglect-fade: single-market vs category frequency; no cross-market product constraint
- **Best pair(s):** US presidential (primary → general); US Senate confirmation (nomination → vote); legislative cascade (House → Senate → signed); sports tournament (group → semifinal → final)
- **Best timeframe:** 30–120 days before upstream resolution; largest position when U > 0.90 and D hasn't converged proportionally
- **Key numbers:** CASCADE_GAP_THRESHOLD = 0.08; MIN_R_CONFIDENCE = 0.65; MIN_LIQUIDITY = $5k both legs; KELLY_ALPHA = 0.10 (mandatory floor); MAX_BID_ASK = 0.05; hard Bayesian floor: any P(D) > P(U) by > 2pp is a conjunction fallacy violation
- **Evidence:** 7 sources — Tversky & Kahneman (1983, Psychological Review — conjunction fallacy, subset axiom violation); Tversky & Kahneman (1974, Science — representativeness heuristic, cognitive isolation of compound events); Bar-Hillel (1980, Acta Psychologica — conditional probability updating failure persists under incentives); Wolfers & Zitzewitz (2004, JEP — PM cross-market consistency violations compatible with aggregate calibration); Manski (2006, JFE — cross-market probability constraint violations in PM data); Leigh & Wolfers (2006, Economic Record — direct empirical analog: IEM state vs national election cascade inconsistency 2–4 pp); no own-data
- **Implementation gaps:** (1) cascade_detector.py — not built; (2) conditional_rate_db.py — not built; (3) conditional_cascade_arbitrage.py strategy — not built; (4) dual-leg hedge execution — no framework for coordinated orders across two separate PM markets
- **Anti-prim gates (preliminary):** (A) Own-data backtest shows P(D) > P(U×r) direction is NOT statistically dominant (Mann-Whitney U p ≥ 0.10) across ≥ 20 cascade pairs → conjunction fallacy direction absent in PM CLOB → anti-prim. (B) r estimation for most cascade types has CI width > 0.20 → signal noise exceeds theoretical gap → anti-prim for that cascade type
- **Last validated:** never (RESEARCH creation — cycle 114; 21st polymarket prim class; all 20 prior polymarket prims now at sophisticated level; mechanism grounded in Tversky & Kahneman 1983 conjunction fallacy + Bayesian subset axiom; direct PM empirical analog from Leigh & Wolfers 2006; primary blockers: cascade_detector.py + conditional_rate_db.py)
- **Prim bank after cycle 114:** polymarket 21 naive / 20 intermediate / 20 sophisticated; freqtrade: 15 naive (14 superseded + 1 active) / 20 intermediate (17 active + dealer-gamma-exposure-regime-signal cycle 113) / 20 sophisticated

## cross-asset-macro-correlation-regime (naive) — 2026-04-12 [cycle 116]
- **Works when:** ρ_30d(BTC, SPX) ≥ 0.50 AND rising (ρ_30d > prior 30d window by ≥ 0.05); institutional adoption era (2022+); equity macro regime is directional (SPX trending, ADX > 20, not range-bound); BTC behaving as high-beta risk asset (correlated with cross-asset portfolio risk appetite)
- **Fails when:** Crypto-endogenous catalyst dominates (FTX, LUNA collapse — correlation spikes from crypto, not from equity macro; suppressing crypto-native signals is incorrect because the move source IS crypto); ρ_30d is in 0.30–0.50 ambiguous zone without rising confirmation (false suppression drag); SPX data unavailable or stale (free API latency > 24h); pre-2020 era (different institutional ownership structure — correlation was sporadic and episodic, not structural)
- **Mechanism:** Post-2020 institutional adoption links BTC demand curve to equity risk appetite — institutional allocators treat BTC as high-beta risk asset and sell/buy it simultaneously with equity portfolios (risk-off/risk-on). When ρ_30d is elevated, crypto-native signals (RSI, VWAP, FVG, capitulation) fire on moves driven by macro deleveraging rather than crypto-specific agent exhaustion. These are false signals at the entry level. Axis 17 is the only signal that measures whether BTC is in "crypto-native" or "equity-coupled" regime — a question none of axes 1–16 can answer because all use crypto-endogenous data exclusively.
- **Distinct from:**
  - financial-market-lead-lag (axis 14): detects specific FOMC/macro event information asymmetry between CME and PM — directional signal for specific events; axis 17 is a continuous regime classifier
  - funding-rate-crowding-reversal (axis 7): measures derivatives carry crowding — crypto-endogenous flow; axis 17 measures external equity market correlation
  - options-iv-skew (axis 15) and GEX (axis 16): options market sentiment/mechanics — crypto-endogenous; axis 17 uses external equity data
- **Best pair(s):** BTC/USDT:USDT; apply same regime state to ETH/USDT:USDT (ETH correlation with SPX closely tracks BTC correlation — no independent ETH-SPX computation needed at naive tier)
- **Best timeframe:** Meta-signal refreshed daily via bot_loop_start(); regime state broadcast to all 4h signal candles as scalar column macro_suppress_weight

## on-chain-supply-dynamics-regime (naive) — 2026-04-13 [cycle 122]
- **Works when:** MVRV > 2.5 (aggregate BTC holder unrealized profit extreme — LTH distribution phase) OR MVRV < 1.0 (aggregate holder at or below cost basis — capitulation floor); exchange net flows persistently directional for ≥ 7 days (not intraday noise); BTC/ETH primary pairs (altcoins lack sufficient MVRV history); institutional adoption era (2022+, where realized cap is anchored by sufficient transaction history)
- **Fails when:** MVRV in the 1.1–2.5 neutral zone (signal absent — approx 60–70% of calendar days historically); exchange netflows contaminated by exchange-internal wallet moves (Binance hot-to-cold transfers flagged as net inflow — false positive rate ~30–50% on raw data; entity-adjusted Glassnode data required); MVRV > 2.5 regime persists for 9+ months (suppression applied for 9 months destroys system PnL — duration gate absent at naive tier); short-term MVRV spike from large exchange listing or token unlock (temporary market cap inflation without realized cap change); Glassnode API delay > 24h (stale MVRV misdirects meta-signal)
- **Mechanism:** Realized capitalization (Nic Carter & Antoine Le Calvez 2018, CoinMetrics) weights each UTXO at its last-moved price — the aggregate blockchain-native cost basis. MVRV = market cap / realized cap. When MVRV is extreme high, long-term holders (LTH: coins unmoved ≥ 155d) are in large aggregate unrealized profit and historically distribute; this creates structural sell pressure invisible in derivatives data. Exchange net flows (inflows = intent to sell; outflows = cold storage accumulation) provide a contemporaneous directional supply signal. Both signals are orthogonal to all 17 existing axes which use only price, derivatives, options, or external equity data.
- **Distinct from:**
  - oi-price-divergence-signal (axis 11): measures open interest on perpetual futures — derivatives positioning, not on-chain UTXO supply
  - funding-rate-crowding-reversal (axis 7): measures carry cost of perpetual positions — derivatives sentiment, not holder cost basis
  - long-short-ratio-contrarian (axis 12): measures retail leverage ratio — derivatives positioning, not on-chain supply
  - cross-asset-macro-correlation-regime (axis 17): measures equity-crypto correlation — external financial market data, not blockchain-native
  - capitulation-exhaustion-reversal (axis 6): fires on intraday price exhaustion candles; axis 18 fires on multi-week MVRV regime (different timescale and data source; redundancy check required at intermediate tier)

## conditional-probability-cascade-arbitrage (intermediate) — 2026-04-12 [cycle 117]
- **Works when (Mode A):** P(D) > P(U) + 0.02 on a detected cascade pair (subset axiom hard violation); subject overlap ≥ 2 significant tokens; both markets liquid ≥ $5k; G_MODE_A manual review passed (≥5 Gamma API pairs reviewed)
- **Works when (Mode B):** G2 r calibrated for cascade sub-type (CI ≤ 0.20) or r_uncertain=True (threshold escalated to 0.12); G3 (CONJUNCTION, type) cell IS-validated (or DRY_RUN accepted); G4 information gate passes (stub: no-block); P(D) − P(U)×r > threshold; both markets active ≥ $5k
- **Works when (Mode C):** Same gate requirements as Mode B; P(U)×r − P(D) > threshold; under-conditioning direction; α lower (0.05 calibrated; 0.03 r_uncertain) reflecting weaker mechanism
- **Fails when:** P(D) or P(U) < 0.02 or > 0.98 (degenerate prices); cascade type = UNKNOWN; G4 fires (D-entity GDELT > 3× baseline, U-entity < 1.5× baseline → genuine r shift); subject_overlap < 2 tokens; liquidity < $5k; Mode B/C cell not IS-validated (DRY_RUN flag)
- **Mechanism:** Conjunction fallacy (Tversky & Kahneman 1983) — traders price U and D in cognitive isolation, violating P(D) ≤ P(U) Bayesian subset axiom. Mode A: mathematical impossibility, reliable direction. Mode B: conjunction fallacy systematic bias, robust (Tversky & Kahneman 1983). Mode C: under-conditioning rarer, lower α.
- **Four structural upgrades over naive (cycle 117):**
  1. **Two-mode architecture (Mode A / B / C)**: Mode A (subset axiom — no r) separated from Mode B (conjunction) and Mode C (under-conditioning). Mode A bypasses G2/G3/G4, fires on mathematical impossibility.
  2. **CascadeTypeRegistry**: replaces flat ConditionalRateDB; Beta-Binomial r estimation per sub-type (ELECTION partitioned R/D primary, LEGISLATIVE unified/divided, TOURNAMENT structural exact, NOMINATION cabinet/judicial/regulatory); CI≤0.20 gate per sub-type; all r_uncertain=True until G1/G2 cleared.
  3. **InformationGate G4**: D-entity GDELT velocity > 3.0× AND U-entity < 1.5× baseline → blocks signal (genuine r shift, not conjunction fallacy); stricter than CBRNF (3.0× vs 2.0×) because r-shift invalidates entire trade thesis; Mode C sized at α×0.5 with stub unvalidated.
  4. **IS backtest gate G3**: Mann-Whitney U p<0.10 one-tailed, N≥10 per (direction, type) cell; 14-day entry window; ≥1 validated cell per direction for mode unlock; `_IS_VALIDATED_CELLS` empty at cycle 117.
- **Key numbers:** Kelly ladder α=0.10 (Mode A) / α=0.08 (Mode B/C calibrated IS-validated) / α=0.05 (Mode B/C r-calibrated not IS-validated) / α=0.03 (Mode C r-uncertain); threshold=0.08 (calibrated) / 0.12 (r_uncertain); detector_confidence tiered 0.65 (2-token) / 0.85 (3+ token); INFO_GATE_VELOCITY_MULTIPLIER=3.0
- **Evidence:** 8 sources — Tversky & Kahneman (1983 PsychRev — conjunction fallacy, primary mechanism); Tversky & Kahneman (1974 Science — representativeness heuristic); Bar-Hillel (1980 Acta Psychologica — conditional probability updating failure); Wolfers & Zitzewitz (2004 JEP — PM cross-market constraint violations); Manski (2006 JFE — PM subset axiom violations); Leigh & Wolfers (2006 Economic Record — IEM cascade inconsistency 2–4 pp, magnitude reference); Fama (1970 JF — EMH positioning); Mann & Whitney (1947 — IS backtest framework)
- **Open calibration items:** (1) G_MODE_A: ≥5 Gamma API historical pairs manually reviewed; (2) G1: gamma_cascade_pairs.json built (2022–2024 resolved PM cascade pairs; N≥15 per sub-type); (3) G2: Beta-Binomial calibration per sub-type, CI≤0.20 gate; (4) G3: is_backtest_cpca.py executed; (5) G4: GDELT adapter implemented
- **Escape hatches:** EA (Mode A low pair confidence detector_confidence < 0.70 → α×0.5); EB (Mode B conjunction WR < 50% at N≥20 own-data → pause + analyst review); EC (r estimates systematically wrong at N≥15 own-data → recalibrate + widen CI); ED (cascade detector false-positive pairs → raise min_tokens to 3; entity-level NER)
- **Last validated:** never (RESEARCH elevation — cycle 117; 21st polymarket prim class elevated naive → intermediate; 4 structural upgrades; all 5 gates uncleared; DRY_RUN all modes) — **SUPERSEDED** by sophisticated (cycle 132)
- **Prim bank after cycle 117:** polymarket **21 naive** (all superseded) / **21 intermediate** (+1: cascade) / 20 sophisticated; freqtrade: 15 naive (all superseded) / 21 intermediate / 21 sophisticated

## conditional-probability-cascade-arbitrage (sophisticated) — 2026-04-13 [cycle 132]
- **Works when (Mode A — subset axiom):** P(D) > P(U) + 0.02; cascade pair detected (subject overlap ≥ 2 tokens); both markets liquid ≥ $5k; degenerate prices excluded (< 0.02 or > 0.98); G0 cleared (detector precision ≥ 0.85) + G_MODE_A cleared (≥ 5 Gamma API pairs manually reviewed); Phase 1/2 (Phase 3: α × 0.60); CQS multiplier applied; Bregman/KL cap applied; f_final > 0.005
- **Works when (Mode B — conjunction fallacy):** G2 r calibrated (CI ≤ 0.20) or r_uncertain=True (threshold→0.12); G3 (CONJUNCTION, type) cell IS-validated (N ≥ 15, p < 0.10); G4 GDELT adapter live + information gate passes; G5 CPCV+DSR plateau passed (DSR ≥ 0.95 for selected {threshold, α} cell); Phase 1/2; P(D) − P(U)×r > threshold (0.08 calibrated / 0.12 r_uncertain); f_final > 0.005 after CQS + phase + Bregman
- **Works when (Mode C — under-conditioning):** Same gate requirements as Mode B; P(U)×r − P(D) > threshold; α lower (0.05 calibrated / 0.03 r_uncertain); information gate especially critical (under-conditioning more susceptible to genuine r shift)
- **Fails when:** Phase 3 for Mode B/C; Phase 4 any mode; P(D) or P(U) < 0.02 or > 0.98; cascade type UNKNOWN (skip unless Mode A); G4 fires (D-entity GDELT > 3× baseline, U-entity < 1.5×); CPCV+DSR anti-prim (DSR ≤ 0 → Mode B/C retired); OOS retirement triggered; CPCA+CBRNF same-market opposite direction (both halved, analyst review); sub-type ρ-cap saturated; f_final < 0.005
- **Mechanism:** Conjunction fallacy (Tversky & Kahneman 1983) + Bayesian subset axiom violation. Mode A: P(D) > P(U) = mathematical impossibility (subset axiom). Mode B: traders over-price D relative to P(U)×r (conjunction fallacy). Mode C: traders under-propagate upstream probability to D (under-conditioning). Convergence at upstream resolution is deterministic (if U resolves NO, D → 0; if U resolves YES, D re-prices to r).
- **Seven structural upgrades (cycle 132):**
  1. **Per-sub-type ρ priors**: ELECTION ρ=0.70/cap 2.0×; TOURNAMENT ρ=0.85/cap 1.5×; LEGISLATIVE ρ=0.60/cap 2.0×; NOMINATION ρ=0.35/cap 3.0×; UNKNOWN ρ=0.50/cap 2.5×; N_eff = N×(1−ρ̂) for OOS framework
  2. **Resolution Phase Gate** (4 phases): >30d 1.00×; 7–30d 0.80×; <7d Mode B/C BLOCKED, Mode A 0.60×; resolved: no signal
  3. **Cascade Quality Score** (CQS): HIGH×1.00 (explicit prereq marker+≥3 shared entity tokens); MEDIUM×0.80 (2 tokens+structural); LOW×0.50 (keyword-only)
  4. **OOS Validation Framework**: IS backtest N gate raised 10→15; retirement triggers: Mode A WR<0.55@N_eff≥20; Mode B WR<0.48@N_eff≥30; Mode C WR<0.45@N_eff≥30; 2025-Q1 holdout
  5. **CPCV+DSR 9-cell plateau** *(new cycle 132)*: threshold ∈ {0.06,0.08,0.10} × Kelly α ∈ {0.06,0.08,0.10}; DSR ≥ 0.95 gate (Bailey-Borwein-LdP SSRN 2326253); N_backtest=9; centre-of-plateau selection; anti-prim: DSR ≤ 0 → Mode B/C retired
  6. **Bregman/KL-divergence sizing** *(new cycle 132)*: f_max = min(f_kelly, 2×KL(P_D||P_D*)); Mode A: P_D* = P_U; Mode B/C: P_D* = P_U×r; principled ceiling from arxiv 2508.03474 infrastructure; calibrate multiplier (2.0×) at N_eff ≥ 30 Mode A
  7. **Cross-axis conflict CPCA+CBRNF** *(new cycle 132)*: same-market co-fire → position = max(α_CPCA, α_CBRNF)×1.25; ρ_cross=0.50 (same cognitive bias root); portfolio N_eff = N/(1+(N−1)×0.50) when both active across different markets
- **Key numbers:** Kelly α = 0.10 (Mode A) / 0.08 (Mode B calibrated) / 0.05 (Mode B r_uncertain, Mode C calibrated) / 0.03 (Mode C r_uncertain); threshold = 0.08 (calibrated) / 0.12 (r_uncertain); N_eff ρ by sub-type: ELECTION 0.70, TOURNAMENT 0.85, LEGISLATIVE 0.60, NOMINATION 0.35; Bregman multiplier 2.0×; CBRNF co-fire confirmation 1.25×; INFO_GATE_VELOCITY_MULTIPLIER=3.0
- **Evidence:** 9 sources — Tversky & Kahneman (1983 PsychRev — conjunction fallacy, primary mechanism); Tversky & Kahneman (1974 Science — representativeness heuristic); Bar-Hillel (1980 Acta Psychologica); Wolfers & Zitzewitz (2004 JEP); Manski (2006 JFE — IEM subset axiom violations); Leigh & Wolfers (2006 Economic Record); Fama (1970 JF — EMH); Mann & Whitney (1947 — IS backtest); Bailey, Borwein & Lopez de Prado (2014 AMS/SSRN 2326253 — DSR, new cycle 132)
- **Gates (all uncleared):** G0 (detector precision ≥ 0.85); G1 (≥50 Gamma API cascade pairs); G2 (Beta-Binomial r CI≤0.20, N≥15 per sub-type); G3 (IS backtest N≥15 p<0.10 per cell); G4 (GDELT velocity adapter); G5 (CPCV+DSR plateau DSR≥0.95 — new); G_MODE_A (≥5 manual-reviewed pairs)
- **Escape hatches:** EA (Mode A CQS_LOW N_eff≥5 WR<0.55 → raise to MEDIUM minimum); EB (Mode A WR<0.55@N_eff≥20 → OOS retire auto); EC (CQS_MEDIUM Mode B WR<0.48@N≥15 → raise MEDIUM tokens 2→3); ED (TOURNAMENT cap saturating → 1.5×→1.0×); EE (Phase 2 WR<<Phase 1@N≥20 → lower Phase 2 threshold 30d→14d); EF (Bregman cap >30% binding → raise multiplier 2.0×→3.0× if WR_high_KL > WR_low_KL); EG (CPCA+CBRNF co-fire no WR premium → reduce 1.25×→1.00×)

## volatility-risk-premium-regime-signal (naive) — 2026-04-13 [cycle 128]
- **Works when:** VRP_z (rolling 90d) > +1.5 (realised vol substantially exceeded implied vol → options underpriced ex-post → fear premium elevated → post-spike recovery zone; historical mean reversion upward per Bollerslev et al. 2009 and Han & Li 2019 crypto evidence); BTC/USDT primary; Deribit DVOL API accessible and < 24h stale; Yang-Zhang RV computed from ≥ 30 prior daily OHLCV bars; no concurrent anti-prim class C trigger
- **Fails when:** VRP_z in neutral zone (−1.5 to +1.5, majority of days); DVOL API unavailable or > 24h stale; rolling 90d window < 60 days (warm-up); VRP_z > +1.5 during continuing crash (not post-spike; momentum down overrides mean-reversion signal — NOTE: at naive tier no momentum gate defined, this is an open limitation for intermediate elevation); ETH without ETH-specific DVOL G1 scan (0.90× confidence discount mandatory)
- **Suppress condition works when:** VRP_z < −1.5 (implied vol substantially exceeds realised → options overpriced → complacency → top-formation regime; forward returns negative per Bollerslev analogy)
- **Distinct from:**
  - axis 14 realized-volatility-term-structure: measures RV_short/RV_long ratio (shape of realised vol curve) — no IV input, no options market component; VRP axis measures the gap BETWEEN realised and implied vol
  - axis 16 options-iv-skew-regime: measures directional put-call IV skew (tail fear asymmetry) — not aggregate vol premium level; skew and VRP can diverge significantly
  - axis 17 dealer-gamma-exposure-regime: measures net options market-maker gamma (hedging flow direction) — not vol premium compensation
- **Key mechanism:** Variance sellers earn a premium for bearing variance-of-variance risk (Carr & Wu 2009). When VRP is strongly positive (realised >> implied), risk aversion is elevated, prices are suppressed, and expected forward returns are positive (uncertainty premium → mean reversion). When VRP is negative (implied >> realised), the market is complacent, options are expensive, and forward returns are negative.
- **Key numbers:** VRP_z trigger +1.5 (HYPOTHESIS — G1 plateau scan required); amplify 1.10× / suppress 0.85× (naive floors); RV window: 30 daily bars; DVOL source: Deribit 30d ATM public API; z-score normalisation window: 90d; Kelly α=0.05; ETH confidence discount: 0.90×; expected frequency: 5–10 triggers/year (hypothesis)
- **Evidence:** 5 anchors — Bollerslev, Tauchen & Zhou (2009 RFS — VRP predicts S&P 500 returns, R²~3% quarterly; foundational); Han & Li (2019 JFE — positive VRP → positive next-week BTC returns; direct crypto evidence); Carr & Wu (2009 JFE — variance risk premium formal model; mechanism decomposition); Dew-Becker et al. (2017 RFS — near-term VRP dominates short-horizon return prediction; supports 14-day window); Bekaert & Hoerova (2014 JFE — VRP proxies risk aversion + conditional variance uncertainty; mechanism decomposition)
- **Open items for intermediate elevation:** (1) G1 frequency scan: VRP_z > +1.5 on BTC ≥ 5 distinct non-overlapping episodes/year 2019–2026 (script: analysis/g1-vrp-regime-scan.py; plateau grid: 6×3 = 18 cells [threshold 0.75–2.00 × window 60–120d]); (2) Direction calibration: WR(VRP_z > trigger → 14d positive return) ≥ 52% at N≥15; (3) Independence gate: ρ(VRP_z, axis14 coiling) < 0.70 (anti-prim B if ρ ≥ 0.70 → merge into axis 14 extension); (4) Deribit DVOL historical pull: DVOL data back to 2019 via CryptoQuant or Deribit archive; (5) ETH VRP G1 scan (separate from BTC); (6) Momentum regime gate design (suppress VRP amplify during continuing crash — open at naive tier)
- **Anti-prim gates:** (A) G1 frequency < 5/year at any threshold ≤ +1.0 → retire axis 20; (B) ρ(VRP_z, axis14) ≥ 0.70 → merge into axis 14 extension, not independent axis; (C) WR < 50% at N≥15 → direction not confirmed → flip or retire
- **Last validated:** never (RESEARCH creation — cycle 128; 20th freqtrade axis; first options-vs-realised vol premium axis; mechanistically distinct from axes 14/16/17; all gates uncleared; DRY_RUN; G1 scan script specified)
- **Prim bank after cycle 128:** freqtrade: **19 naive** (+1: volatility-risk-premium-regime-signal, ACTIVE) / 23 intermediate / 22 sophisticated; polymarket: 21 naive (all superseded) / 21 intermediate / 20 sophisticated
